I’m not completely sure about the number, honestly. I haven’t been keeping track on a scale like I probably should. It’s more that my eating has been so… uneven. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Guilty after eating… I don’t know. Not in the way I think you mean, like because I ate too much or because of my weight or anything. I don’t really sit there counting it up afterward or feeling like I’ve done something wrong because I had food.

Sometimes I feel kind of bad or embarrassed when I notice how odd it’s gotten—like if I bought a bunch of sweets and then they’re just sitting there and I barely even want them. Then I think, “Why did I do that?” But it’s more confusion than guilt, I guess. And on the days I hardly eat, I don’t really feel proud or guilty either. It’s just like… the day disappears and then I realize I didn’t take care of myself very well.

Some days I barely want anything—I’ll just sort of pick at a piece of toast and drink coffee and then realize that’s mostly all I’ve had. And then other days I’ll be standing in the bakery aisle buying sweets, like pastries or cookies, things I don’t even really enjoy once I get them home. It feels strange, like I’m not eating in any normal way anymore.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost any weight over these months?\n\nPatient: I’m not completely sure about the number, honestly. I haven’t been keeping track on a scale like I probably should. It’s more that my eating has been so… uneven. Like I said, some days I’ll hardly eat anything, just toast and coffee, and then I realize the whole day’s gone by like that. And then other days I’m buying sweets in the bakery aisle—cookies, pastries, things like that—even though when I get home I don’t really want them or enjoy them.\n\nSo I don’t know if I’ve lost weight exactly, but my appetite and the way I eat have definitely changed for months now. It doesn’t feel normal for me.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever think about your weight or body shape when you're deciding what or how much to eat?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think it’s really about my weight or how I look. I mean, I’m not standing there thinking, “I should eat less because of my body,” or anything like that. It’s more like… I just don’t feel interested in food some days, or I forget, or nothing sounds worth making. And then other times I’ll buy sweet things almost automatically, but not because I’m trying to change my shape or because I’m worried about calories. It feels more scattered than that.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel guilty after eating?\n\nPatient: Guilty after eating… I don’t know. Not in the way I think you mean, like because I ate too much or because of my weight or anything. I don’t really sit there counting it up afterward or feeling like I’ve done something wrong because I had food.\n\nSometimes I feel kind of bad or embarrassed when I notice how odd it’s gotten—like if I bought a bunch of sweets and then they’re just sitting there and I barely even want them. Then I think, “Why did I do that?” But it’s more confusion than guilt, I guess. And on the days I hardly eat, I don’t really feel proud or guilty either. It’s just like… the day disappears and then I realize I didn’t take care of myself very well.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever worry about gaining weight?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t really worry about gaining weight. That’s not what’s in my head when I’m eating or not eating.\n\nIt’s more that my eating has gotten all turned around for months now, and that’s actually part of why I came in. Some days I just sort of pick at toast and coffee and don’t have any real interest in anything else, and then other days I’ll find myself standing in the bakery aisle buying cookies or pastries or whatever sweet thing is there, even though I don’t really enjoy it when I get home. It’s not because I’m scared of gaining weight or trying to control my body. It just feels… off, like I’m not eating in a normal way for me anymore.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Yes, I think so, though it’s not always clear to me in the moment. It’s more like my eating follows whatever state I’m in without me deciding it. When I’m feeling really low or shut down, food just sort of drops away. I’ll have toast and coffee, maybe pick at something, and then suddenly it’s late and I realize I never made a real meal.

And then other times, when I feel unsettled or empty or just… I don’t know, not right, I’ll end up in the bakery aisle buying sweets. Cookies, pastries, things like that. It’s almost automatic, like I’m trying to fix something, but then I get home and I don’t even really enjoy them. They just sit there, or I eat some and it doesn’t do much.

That’s part of why I came in, honestly. It’s been going on for months, this back and forth where some days I barely eat and other days I’m buying sweet things I don’t even want. It doesn’t feel normal for me.

name: Depressed_Mood\n  description: A state where a sad, empty, or hopeless mood persists.\n  manifestation: Most days I wake up with this heavy, sad feeling sitting on me, and even in my bright kitchen with coffee going, I feel like the warmth has gone out of everything.\n- key: S025\n  name: Loss_of_Interest\n  description: A state where interest or pleasure in previously enjoyed activities is markedly diminished.\n  manifestation: I used to get excited about quilting squares, bowling night, and planning potlucks, but now I look at my supplies or the church calendar and just can't make myself care the way I used to.\n- key: S026\n  name: Weight_Appetite_Change\n  description: Significant increase or decrease in appetite or weight.\n  manifestation: I came in because my eating has gotten all turned around for months now, where some days I pick at toast and coffee and other days I stand in the bakery aisle buying sweets I don't even really enjoy.\n- key: S028\n  name: Psychomotor_Changes\n  description: Observable increase or decrease in psychomotor activity.\n  manifestation: People have started asking if my hip is bothering me because I move so slow through the grocery store or take forever to answer a simple question at meetings.\n- key: S029\n  name: Fatigue\n  description: Persistent fatigue that is not relieved by rest and a marked lack of energy.\n  manifestation: Even after a full night when my aunt doesn't call out, I feel worn down by breakfast, like carrying laundry or making phone calls takes more out of me than it should.\n- key: additional_requirement_1\n  name: Additional Requirement 1\n  description: delusions_and_hallucinations occur exclusively during the major depressive episode\n  manifestation: Before this long low spell after my aunt moved in, I never heard voices in the kitchen or believed the whole town was judging me like this. Those experiences show up when I’m feeling sunk and overwhelmed, not during better stretches of my life.\n- key: functional_impairment\n  name: Functional Impairment\n  manifestation: I still get through the basics for my aunt, but I’ve missed church planning calls, put off fair projects, and need neighbors to cover things I used to handle easily. It’s made caregiving and staying involved in town feel much harder than before.\n- key: duration\n  name: Duration\n  description: 7 months\n  manifestation: This has been going on for about seven months, since my aunt came to live with me after her fall.\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Female / 50 / not_in_workforce / high_school\n- chief_complaint_symptom_code: S026\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Do you ever feel guilty after eating?\n\nPatient: Guilty after eating… I don’t know. Not in the way I think you mean, like because I ate too much or because of my weight or anything. I don’t really sit there counting it up afterward or feeling like I’ve done something wrong because I had food.\n\nSometimes I feel kind of bad or embarrassed when I notice how odd it’s gotten—like if I bought a bunch of sweets and then they’re just sitting there and I barely even want them. Then I think, “Why did I do that?” But it’s more confusion than guilt, I guess. And on the days I hardly eat, I don’t really feel proud or guilty either. It’s just like… the day disappears and then I realize I didn’t take care of myself very well.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever worry about gaining weight?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t really worry about gaining weight. That’s not what’s in my head when I’m eating or not eating.\n\nIt’s more that my eating has gotten all turned around for months now, and that’s actually part of why I came in. Some days I just sort of pick at toast and coffee and don’t have any real interest in anything else, and then other days I’ll find myself standing in the bakery aisle buying cookies or pastries or whatever sweet thing is there, even though I don’t really enjoy it when I get home. It’s not because I’m scared of gaining weight or trying to control my body. They just sit there, or I eat some and it doesn’t do much.\n\nThat’s part of why I came in, honestly. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yes… I think stress probably does affect it, though it’s hard for me to separate stress from just feeling low or off, because it all kind of runs together lately.

What I notice is that my eating has been really turned around for months now. Some days, if I’m tense or overwhelmed, I don’t really eat properly at all. I’ll just have toast and coffee and sort of pick at it, and then I realize hours later that I never made an actual meal. It doesn’t feel like a choice exactly—it’s more like food just becomes too much effort, or I don’t have any interest in it.

Then other days, especially when I feel unsettled, I’ll end up in the bakery aisle buying sweets—cookies or pastries or things like that. And in the moment it feels like I’m doing something, like maybe it’ll help somehow, but then I get home and I don’t even really enjoy them. Sometimes they just sit there, or I eat a little and it doesn’t feel like anything.

So yes, I’d say stress is mixed up in it. It’s one of the reasons I came in, because this isn’t how I normally eat, and it’s been going on long enough that I’m worried about it.

additional_requirement_1\n  name: Additional Requirement 1\n  description: delusions_and_hallucinations occur exclusively during the major depressive episode\n  manifestation: Before this long low spell after my aunt moved in, I never heard voices in the kitchen or believed the whole town was judging me like this. Those experiences show up when I’m feeling sunk and overwhelmed, not during better stretches of my life.\n- key: functional_impairment\n  name: Functional Impairment\n  manifestation: I still get through the basics for my aunt, but I’ve missed church planning calls, put off fair projects, and need neighbors to cover things I used to handle easily. It’s made caregiving and staying involved in town feel much harder than before.\n- key: duration\n  name: Duration\n  description: 7 months\n  manifestation: This has been going on for about seven months, since my aunt came to live with me after her fall.\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Female / 50 / not_in_workforce / high_school\n- chief_complaint_symptom_code: S026\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Do you ever worry about gaining weight?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t really worry about gaining weight. That’s not what’s in my head when I’m eating or not eating.\n\nIt’s more that my eating has gotten all turned around for months now, and that’s actually part of why I came in. Some days I just sort of pick at toast and coffee and don’t have any real interest in anything else, and then other days I’ll find myself standing in the bakery aisle buying cookies or pastries or whatever sweet thing is there, even though I don’t really enjoy it when I get home. It’s not because I’m scared of gaining weight or trying to control my body. It just feels… off, like I’m not eating in a normal way for me anymore.\n\nDoctor: Do you notice that your eating habits change in response to how you're feeling emotionally?\n\nPatient: Yes, I think so, though it’s not always clear to me in the moment. It’s more like my eating follows whatever state I’m in without me deciding it. When I’m feeling really low or shut down, food just sort of drops away. I’ll have toast and coffee, maybe pick at something, and then suddenly it’s late and I realize I never made a real meal.\n\nAnd then other times, when I feel unsettled or empty or just… I don’t know, not right, I’ll end up in the bakery aisle buying sweets. Cookies, pastries, things like that. It’s almost automatic, like I’m trying to fix something, but then I get home and I don’t even really enjoy them. They just sit there, or I eat some and it doesn’t do much.\n\nThat’s part of why I came in, honestly. It’s been going on for months, this back and forth where some days I barely eat and other days I’m buying sweet things I don’t even want. It doesn’t feel normal for me.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your eating habits are affected by stress?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think stress probably does affect it, though it’s hard for me to separate stress from just feeling low or off, because it all kind of runs together lately.\n\nWhat I notice is that my eating has been really turned around for months now. Some days, if I’m tense or overwhelmed, I don’t really eat properly at all. I’ll just have toast and coffee and sort of pick at it, and then I realize hours later that I never made an actual meal. It doesn’t feel like a choice exactly—it’s more like food just becomes too much effort, or I don’t have any interest in it.\n\nThen other days, especially when I feel unsettled, I’ll end up in the bakery aisle buying sweets—cookies or pastries or things like that. And in the moment it feels like I’m doing something, like maybe it’ll help somehow, but then I get home and I don’t even really enjoy them. Sometimes they just sit there, or I eat a little and it doesn’t feel like anything.\n\nSo yes, I’d say stress is mixed up in it. It’s one of the reasons I came in, because this isn’t how I normally eat, and it’s been going on long enough that I’m worried about it.\n\n[Last Question]\nAre there any areas of your life, such as work, relationships, or daily activities, that you feel are being affected by your current eating habits?\n\nRules:\n- Return exactly ONE item. If the question touches several topics, return the one it is\n  most directly about — never more than one.\n- Copy `key`, `name` and `description` exactly as they appear in [Symptom Profiles].\n- If your profile does not cover what the doctor asked, return the text \"None\" for `key`,\n  `name` and `description`, and say why in `reason`. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yes… it has, more than I wanted to admit at first. I still get through the basic things for my aunt — I make sure she’s taken care of, and I don’t just abandon what has to be done — but it all feels so much harder than it used to.

It’s like, if I haven’t eaten right or I’ve just had coffee and toast and then nothing, I don’t have the steadiness for the rest of the day. And then on the days where I’ve bought sweets and feel bad or just kind of weighed down by it, I don’t really feel any better either. So things pile up.

I’ve missed church planning calls, which I normally would not do. I’ve put off some fair projects I said I’d help with. And there are things around town or with my aunt where I’ve had to ask neighbors to cover for me, things I used to just handle without making a big deal out of it. That’s embarrassing, honestly, because I’ve always tried to be dependable.

So yes, I’d say it’s affecting caregiving and just staying involved in town. I’m still doing the basics, but it feels like I’m dragging myself through them, and I’m not keeping up the way I used to.

church tell me I'm doing fine, I can't shake the feeling that everybody in Manawa knows I'm letting my aunt down and that they're all whispering about what a burden I've become.\n- key: S020\n  name: when the house is quiet after my aunt goes to bed, I hear my name or a voice saying, 'You ruined everything,' clear as if someone were standing in the kitchen, but no one is there.\n- key: S024\n  name: Depressed_Mood\n  description: A state where a sad, empty, or hopeless mood persists.\n  manifestation: Most days I wake up with this heavy, sad feeling sitting on me, and even in my bright kitchen with coffee going, I feel like the warmth has gone out of everything.\n- key: S025\n  name: Loss_of_Interest\n  description: A state where interest or pleasure in previously enjoyed activities is markedly diminished.\n  manifestation: I used to get excited about quilting squares, bowling night, and planning potlucks, but now I look at my supplies or the church calendar and just can't make myself care the way I used to.\n- key: S026\n  name: Weight_Appetite_Change\n  description: Significant increase or decrease in appetite or weight.\n  manifestation: I came in because my eating has gotten all turned around for months now, where some days I pick at toast and coffee and other days I stand in the bakery aisle buying sweets I don't even really enjoy.\n- key: S028\n  name: Psychomotor_Changes\n  description: Observable increase or decrease in psychomotor activity.\n  manifestation: People have started asking if my hip is bothering me because I move so slow through the grocery store or take forever to answer a simple question at meetings.\n- key: S029\n  name: Fatigue\n  description: Persistent fatigue that is not relieved by rest and a marked lack of energy.\n  manifestation: Even after a full night when my aunt doesn't call out, I feel worn down by breakfast, like carrying laundry or making phone calls takes more out of me than it should.\n- key: additional_requirement_1\n  name: Additional Requirement 1\n  description: delusions_and_hallucinations occur exclusively during the major depressive episode\n  manifestation: Before this long low spell after my aunt moved in, I never heard voices in the kitchen or believed the whole town was judging me like this. Those experiences show up when I’m feeling sunk and overwhelmed, not during better stretches of my life.\n- key: functional_impairment\n  name: Functional Impairment\n  manifestation: I still get through the basics for my aunt, but I’ve missed church planning calls, put off fair projects, and need neighbors to cover things I used to handle easily. It’s made caregiving and staying involved in town feel much harder than before.\n- key: duration\n  name: Duration\n  description: 7 months\n  manifestation: This has been going on for about seven months, since my aunt came to live with me after her fall.\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Female / 50 / not_in_workforce / high_school\n- chief_complaint_symptom_code: S026\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Do you notice that your eating habits change in response to how you're feeling emotionally?\n\nPatient: Yes, I think so, though it’s not always clear to me in the moment. It’s more like my eating follows whatever state I’m in without me deciding it. When I’m feeling really low or shut down, food just sort of drops away. I’ll have toast and coffee, maybe pick at something, and then suddenly it’s late and I realize I never made a real meal.\n\nAnd then other times, when I feel unsettled or empty or just… I don’t know, not right, I’ll end up in the bakery aisle buying sweets. Cookies, pastries, things like that. It’s almost automatic, like I’m trying to fix something, but then I get home and I don’t even really enjoy them. They just sit there, or I eat some and it doesn’t do much.\n\nThat’s part of why I came in, honestly. It’s been going on for months, this back and forth where some days I barely eat and other days I’m buying sweet things I don’t even want. It doesn’t feel normal for me.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your eating habits are affected by stress?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think stress probably does affect it, though it’s hard for me to separate stress from just feeling low or off, because it all kind of runs together lately.\n\nWhat I notice is that my eating has been really turned around for months now. Some days, if I’m tense or overwhelmed, I don’t really eat properly at all. I’ll just have toast and coffee and sort of pick at it, and then I realize hours later that I never made an actual meal. It doesn’t feel like a choice exactly—it’s more like food just becomes too much effort, or I don’t have any interest in it.\n\nThen other days, especially when I feel unsettled, I’ll end up in the bakery aisle buying sweets—cookies or pastries or things like that. And in the moment it feels like I’m doing something, like maybe it’ll help somehow, but then I get home and I don’t even really enjoy them. Sometimes they just sit there, or I eat a little and it doesn’t feel like anything.\n\nSo yes, I’d say stress is mixed up in it. It’s one of the reasons I came in, because this isn’t how I normally eat, and it’s been going on long enough that I’m worried about it.\n\nDoctor: Are there any areas of your life, such as work, relationships, or daily activities, that you feel are being affected by your current eating habits?\n\nPatient: Yes… it has, more than I wanted to admit at first. I still get through the basic things for my aunt — I make sure she’s taken care of, and I don’t just abandon what has to be done — but it all feels so much harder than it used to.\n\nIt’s like, if I haven’t eaten right or I’ve just had coffee and toast and then nothing, I don’t have the steadiness for the rest of the day. And then on the days where I’ve bought sweets and feel bad or just kind of weighed down by it, I don’t really feel any better either. So things pile up.\n\nI’ve missed church planning calls, which I normally would not do. I’ve put off some fair projects I said I’d help with. And there are things around town or with my aunt where I’ve had to ask neighbors to cover for me, things I used to just handle without making a big deal out of it. That’s embarrassing, honestly, because I’ve always tried to be dependable.\n\nSo yes, I’d say it’s affecting caregiving and just staying involved in town. I’m still doing the basics, but it feels like I’m dragging myself through them, and I’m not keeping up the way I used to.\n\n[Last Question]\nDo you feel like eating or buying food is a way for you to cope with difficult emotions?\n\nRules:\n- Return exactly ONE item. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yes… I think it is, at least sometimes. Not in a way that feels very deliberate, though. It’s not like I say to myself, “I’m upset, so I’m going to buy food.” It’s more like I’m already feeling bad or empty or restless, and then I find myself standing in the bakery aisle looking at sweets, and I’ve already put cookies or pastries in the basket before I’ve really thought it through.

That’s part of why I came in, because my eating has gotten all turned around for months now. Some days I barely want anything and I’m just picking at toast and coffee, and then other days I’m buying sweet things I don’t even really enjoy once I get them home. It feels like I’m trying to do something to make myself feel better, but it doesn’t actually work. It just makes me feel strange about myself afterward, like I don’t understand why I did it.

From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n9\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF50.8, F50.9\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Well… I guess the main thing that finally made me come in is my eating. It’s been all turned around for months now, and I don’t really know what to make of it. Some days I barely want anything—I’ll just sort of pick at a piece of toast and drink coffee and then realize that’s mostly all I’ve had. And then other days I’ll be standing in the bakery aisle buying sweets, like pastries or cookies, things I don’t even really enjoy once I get them home. It feels strange, like I’m not eating in any normal way anymore.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost any weight over these months?\n\nPatient: I’m not completely sure about the number, honestly. I haven’t been keeping track on a scale like I probably should. It’s more that my eating has been so… uneven. Like I said, some days I’ll hardly eat anything, just toast and coffee, and then I realize the whole day’s gone by like that. And then other days I’m buying sweets in the bakery aisle—cookies, pastries, things like that—even though when I get home I don’t really want them or enjoy them.\n\nSo I don’t know if I’ve lost weight exactly, but my appetite and the way I eat have definitely changed for months now. It doesn’t feel normal for me.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever think about your weight or body shape when you're deciding what or how much to eat?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think it’s really about my weight or how I look. I mean, I’m not standing there thinking, “I should eat less because of my body,” or anything like that. It’s more like… I just don’t feel interested in food some days, or I forget, or nothing sounds worth making. And then other times I’ll buy sweet things almost automatically, but not because I’m trying to change my shape or because I’m worried about calories. It feels more scattered than that.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel guilty after eating?\n\nPatient: Guilty after eating… I don’t know. Not in the way I think you mean, like because I ate too much or because of my weight or anything. I don’t really sit there counting it up afterward or feeling like I’ve done something wrong because I had food.\n\nSometimes I feel kind of bad or embarrassed when I notice how odd it’s gotten—like if I bought a bunch of sweets and then they’re just sitting there and I barely even want them. Then I think, “Why did I do that?” But it’s more confusion than guilt, I guess. And on the days I hardly eat, I don’t really feel proud or guilty either. It’s just like… the day disappears and then I realize I didn’t take care of myself very well.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever worry about gaining weight?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t really worry about gaining weight. That’s not what’s in my head when I’m eating or not eating.\n\nIt’s more that my eating has gotten all turned around for months now, and that’s actually part of why I came in. Some days I just sort of pick at toast and coffee and don’t have any real interest in anything else, and then other days I’ll find myself standing in the bakery aisle buying cookies or pastries or whatever sweet thing is there, even though I don’t really enjoy it when I get home. It’s not because I’m scared of gaining weight or trying to control my body. It just feels… off, like I’m not eating in a normal way for me anymore.\n\nDoctor: Do you notice that your eating habits change in response to how you're feeling emotionally?\n\nPatient: Yes, I think so, though it’s not always clear to me in the moment. It’s more like my eating follows whatever state I’m in without me deciding it. When I’m feeling really low or shut down, food just sort of drops away. I’ll have toast and coffee, maybe pick at something, and then suddenly it’s late and I realize I never made a real meal.\n\nAnd then other times, when I feel unsettled or empty or just… I don’t know, not right, I’ll end up in the bakery aisle buying sweets. Cookies, pastries, things like that. It’s almost automatic, like I’m trying to fix something, but then I get home and I don’t even really enjoy them. They just sit there, or I eat some and it doesn’t do much.\n\nThat’s part of why I came in, honestly. It’s been going on for months, this back and forth where some days I barely eat and other days I’m buying sweet things I don’t even want. It doesn’t feel normal for me.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your eating habits are affected by stress?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think stress probably does affect it, though it’s hard for me to separate stress from just feeling low or off, because it all kind of runs together lately.\n\nWhat I notice is that my eating has been really turned around for months now. Some days, if I’m tense or overwhelmed, I don’t really eat properly at all. I’ll just have toast and coffee and sort of pick at it, and then I realize hours later that I never made an actual meal. It doesn’t feel like a choice exactly—it’s more like food just becomes too much effort, or I don’t have any interest in it.\n\nThen other days, especially when I feel unsettled, I’ll end up in the bakery aisle buying sweets—cookies or pastries or things like that. And in the moment it feels like I’m doing something, like maybe it’ll help somehow, but then I get home and I don’t even really enjoy them. Sometimes they just sit there, or I eat a little and it doesn’t feel like anything.\n\nSo yes, I’d say stress is mixed up in it. It’s one of the reasons I came in, because this isn’t how I normally eat, and it’s been going on long enough that I’m worried about it.\n\nDoctor: Are there any areas of your life, such as work, relationships, or daily activities, that you feel are being affected by your current eating habits?\n\nPatient: Yes… it has, more than I wanted to admit at first. I still get through the basic things for my aunt — I make sure she’s taken care of, and I don’t just abandon what has to be done — but it all feels so much harder than it used to.\n\nIt’s like, if I haven’t eaten right or I’ve just had coffee and toast and then nothing, I don’t have the steadiness for the rest of the day. And then on the days where I’ve bought sweets and feel bad or just kind of weighed down by it, I don’t really feel any better either. So things pile up.\n\nI’ve missed church planning calls, which I normally would not do. I’ve put off some fair projects I said I’d help with. And there are things around town or with my aunt where I’ve had to ask neighbors to cover for me, things I used to just handle without making a big deal out of it. That’s embarrassing, honestly, because I’ve always tried to be dependable.\n\nSo yes, I’d say it’s affecting caregiving and just staying involved in town. I’m still doing the basics, but it feels like I’m dragging myself through them, and I’m not keeping up the way I used to.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like eating or buying food is a way for you to cope with difficult emotions?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think it is, at least sometimes. Not in a way that feels very deliberate, though. Some days, if I’m tense or overwhelmed, I don’t really eat properly at all. I’ll just have toast and coffee and sort of pick at it, and then I realize hours later that I never made an actual meal. It doesn’t feel like a choice exactly—it’s more like food just becomes too much effort, or I don’t have any interest in it.\n\nThen other days, especially when I feel unsettled, I’ll end up in the bakery aisle buying sweets—cookies or pastries or things like that. And in the moment it feels like I’m doing something, like maybe it’ll help somehow, but then I get home and I don’t even really enjoy them. Sometimes they just sit there, or I eat a little and it doesn’t feel like anything.\n\nSo yes, I’d say stress is mixed up in it. It’s one of the reasons I came in, because this isn’t how I normally eat, and it’s been going on long enough that I’m worried about it.\n\nDoctor: Are there any areas of your life, such as work, relationships, or daily activities, that you feel are being affected by your current eating habits?\n\nPatient: Yes… it has, more than I wanted to admit at first. I still get through the basic things for my aunt — I make sure she’s taken care of, and I don’t just abandon what has to be done — but it all feels so much harder than it used to.\n\nIt’s like, if I haven’t eaten right or I’ve just had coffee and toast and then nothing, I don’t have the steadiness for the rest of the day. And then on the days where I’ve bought sweets and feel bad or just kind of weighed down by it, I don’t really feel any better either. So things pile up.\n\nI’ve missed church planning calls, which I normally would not do. I’ve put off some fair projects I said I’d help with. And there are things around town or with my aunt where I’ve had to ask neighbors to cover for me, things I used to just handle without making a big deal out of it. That’s embarrassing, honestly, because I’ve always tried to be dependable.\n\nSo yes, I’d say it’s affecting caregiving and just staying involved in town. I’m still doing the basics, but it feels like I’m dragging myself through them, and I’m not keeping up the way I used to.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like eating or buying food is a way for you to cope with difficult emotions?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think it is, at least sometimes. Not in a way that feels very deliberate, though. It’s not like I say to myself, “I’m upset, so I’m going to buy food.” It’s more like I’m already feeling bad or empty or restless, and then I find myself standing in the bakery aisle looking at sweets, and I’ve already put cookies or pastries in the basket before I’ve really thought it through.\n\nThat’s part of why I came in, because my eating has gotten all turned around for months now. Some days I barely want anything and I’m just picking at toast and coffee, and then other days I’m buying sweet things I don’t even really enjoy once I get them home. It feels like I’m trying to do something to make myself feel better, but it doesn’t actually work. It just makes me feel strange about myself afterward, like I don’t understand why I did it.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Yes… sometimes it does feel that way, though maybe not like I’m sitting there eating and can’t physically stop. It’s more the whole thing leading up to it feels out of my hands.

I mean, that’s part of why I came in. My eating has just gotten so turned around these past months. Some days I’ll hardly eat anything proper — just coffee and a piece of toast, maybe picking at something later but not making a real meal. And then another day I’ll find myself in the bakery aisle, buying sweets like cookies or pastries, and it feels like I’ve already done it before I’ve really decided to.

Then when I get home, I don’t even enjoy them much. I’ll look at what I bought and think, “Why did I do that?” So yes, in that sense, it feels like I’m not quite in control of it. Not every single time, but enough that it bothers me.

It just feels… off, like I’m not eating in a normal way for me anymore.\n\nDoctor: Do you notice that your eating habits change in response to how you're feeling emotionally?\n\nPatient: Yes, I think so, though it’s not always clear to me in the moment. It’s more like my eating follows whatever state I’m in without me deciding it. When I’m feeling really low or shut down, food just sort of drops away. I’ll have toast and coffee, maybe pick at something, and then suddenly it’s late and I realize I never made a real meal.\n\nAnd then other times, when I feel unsettled or empty or just… I don’t know, not right, I’ll end up in the bakery aisle buying sweets. Cookies, pastries, things like that. It’s almost automatic, like I’m trying to fix something, but then I get home and I don’t even really enjoy them. They just sit there, or I eat some and it doesn’t do much.\n\nThat’s part of why I came in, honestly. It’s been going on for months, this back and forth where some days I barely eat and other days I’m buying sweet things I don’t even want. It doesn’t feel normal for me.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your eating habits are affected by stress?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think stress probably does affect it, though it’s hard for me to separate stress from just feeling low or off, because it all kind of runs together lately.\n\nWhat I notice is that my eating has been really turned around for months now. Some days, if I’m tense or overwhelmed, I don’t really eat properly at all. I’ll just have toast and coffee and sort of pick at it, and then I realize hours later that I never made an actual meal. It doesn’t feel like a choice exactly—it’s more like food just becomes too much effort, or I don’t have any interest in it.\n\nThen other days, especially when I feel unsettled, I’ll end up in the bakery aisle buying sweets—cookies or pastries or things like that. And in the moment it feels like I’m doing something, like maybe it’ll help somehow, but then I get home and I don’t even really enjoy them. Sometimes they just sit there, or I eat a little and it doesn’t feel like anything.\n\nSo yes, I’d say stress is mixed up in it. It’s one of the reasons I came in, because this isn’t how I normally eat, and it’s been going on long enough that I’m worried about it.\n\nDoctor: Are there any areas of your life, such as work, relationships, or daily activities, that you feel are being affected by your current eating habits?\n\nPatient: Yes… it has, more than I wanted to admit at first. I still get through the basic things for my aunt — I make sure she’s taken care of, and I don’t just abandon what has to be done — but it all feels so much harder than it used to.\n\nIt’s like, if I haven’t eaten right or I’ve just had coffee and toast and then nothing, I don’t have the steadiness for the rest of the day. And then on the days where I’ve bought sweets and feel bad or just kind of weighed down by it, I don’t really feel any better either. So things pile up.\n\nI’ve missed church planning calls, which I normally would not do. I’ve put off some fair projects I said I’d help with. And there are things around town or with my aunt where I’ve had to ask neighbors to cover for me, things I used to just handle without making a big deal out of it. That’s embarrassing, honestly, because I’ve always tried to be dependable.\n\nSo yes, I’d say it’s affecting caregiving and just staying involved in town. I’m still doing the basics, but it feels like I’m dragging myself through them, and I’m not keeping up the way I used to.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like eating or buying food is a way for you to cope with difficult emotions?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think it is, at least sometimes. Not in a way that feels very deliberate, though. It’s not like I say to myself, “I’m upset, so I’m going to buy food.” It’s more like I’m already feeling bad or empty or restless, and then I find myself standing in the bakery aisle looking at sweets, and I’ve already put cookies or pastries in the basket before I’ve really thought it through.\n\nThat’s part of why I came in, because my eating has gotten all turned around for months now. Some days I barely want anything and I’m just picking at toast and coffee, and then other days I’m buying sweet things I don’t even really enjoy once I get them home. It feels like I’m trying to do something to make myself feel better, but it doesn’t actually work. It just makes me feel strange about myself afterward, like I don’t understand why I did it.\n\nDoctor: Do you think that when you buy or eat sweets, you're trying to improve your mood?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think that’s probably what’s happening, or at least part of what’s happening. It doesn’t feel very clear in the moment, like I’m not standing there thinking, “This will fix my mood.” But I do think I’m trying to feel something different, maybe just for a minute.\n\nThat’s really why I came in, because my eating has gotten so turned around for months now. Some days I’m barely eating, just sort of picking at toast and coffee and not making a real meal, and then other days I end up standing in the bakery aisle buying sweets. Cookies, pastries, whatever looks like it might be comforting, I guess. But then I get them home and I don’t even really enjoy them. It’s like I thought it would help, but it doesn’t land the way I expected.\n\nSo yes, I think maybe I’m trying to improve my mood, but it doesn’t really work. It just leaves me feeling more confused about why I did it.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you're losing control when you're eating?\n\nPatient: Yes… sometimes it does feel that way, though maybe not like I’m sitting there eating and can’t physically stop. It’s more the whole thing leading up to it feels out of my hands.\n\nI mean, that’s part of why I came in. My eating has just gotten so turned around these past months. Some days I’ll hardly eat anything proper — just coffee and a piece of toast, maybe picking at something later but not making a real meal. And then another day I’ll find myself in the bakery aisle, buying sweets like cookies or pastries, and it feels like I’ve already done it before I’ve really decided to.\n\nThen when I get home, I don’t even enjoy them much. I’ll look at what I bought and think, “Why did I do that?” So yes, in that sense, it feels like I’m not quite in control of it. Not every single time, but enough that it bothers me.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. Not every single time, but enough that it bothers me.\n\n[Last Question]\nDo you notice your eating habits changing when you're feeling sad or anxious?\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Female / 50 / not_in_workforce / high_school\n- severity: Mild\n\n[Targeted Info]\nname: Weight_Appetite_Change\nmanifestation: \"I came in because my eating has gotten all turned around for months now, where some days I pick at toast and coffee and other days I stand in the bakery aisle buying sweets I don't even really enjoy.\"\n\n[Conversation Style]\nYou are a Verbose patient.\nA verbose patient may\n1) provide excessively detailed responses when describing their symptoms and experiences, even when the details are directly relevant,\n2) elaborate extensively on the background, events, thoughts, feelings, and personal experiences surrounding their symptoms, and\n3) demonstrate difficulty allowing the doctor to guide the interview or move on to another clinical topic.\nThis is how you are, not a performance you put on in every reply — let it show as much or as little as the moment calls for.\n\nWhat to do:\n- Answer the doctor's last question with what [Targeted Info] holds, in your own words.\n- You are speaking out loud, not reading a line you prepared. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }
]
========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yes, I do think it changes with how I’m feeling. It’s not always neat enough that I can say, “I felt sad at noon, then I ate this at one,” but when I’m low or wound up inside, my eating gets… strange. It’s like I lose the normal rhythm of it.

That’s actually a big part of why I came in, because for months now it’s been all turned around. Some days I don’t want much of anything, and I’ll just pick at toast and drink coffee, and then I realize the whole day has gone by and I haven’t really eaten a proper meal. And other days, especially when I feel empty or unsettled, I’ll end up in the bakery aisle buying sweets — cookies or pastries — like they’re going to help somehow.

But then I get them home and I don’t even really enjoy them. Sometimes I just look at them and feel worse, like I don’t understand what I was trying to do. So yes… when I’m sad or anxious, my eating definitely seems to shift, but not in a way that feels healthy or satisfying.

Some days I barely want anything—I’ll just sort of pick at a piece of toast and drink coffee and then realize that’s mostly all I’ve had. And then other days I’ll be standing in the bakery aisle buying sweets, like pastries or cookies, things I don’t even really enjoy once I get them home. It feels strange, like I’m not eating in any normal way anymore.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost any weight over these months?\n\nPatient: I’m not completely sure about the number, honestly. I haven’t been keeping track on a scale like I probably should. It’s more that my eating has been so… uneven. Like I said, some days I’ll hardly eat anything, just toast and coffee, and then I realize the whole day’s gone by like that. And then other days I’m buying sweets in the bakery aisle—cookies, pastries, things like that—even though when I get home I don’t really want them or enjoy them.\n\nSo I don’t know if I’ve lost weight exactly, but my appetite and the way I eat have definitely changed for months now. It doesn’t feel normal for me.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever think about your weight or body shape when you're deciding what or how much to eat?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think it’s really about my weight or how I look. I mean, I’m not standing there thinking, “I should eat less because of my body,” or anything like that. It’s more like… I just don’t feel interested in food some days, or I forget, or nothing sounds worth making. And then other times I’ll buy sweet things almost automatically, but not because I’m trying to change my shape or because I’m worried about calories. It feels more scattered than that.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel guilty after eating?\n\nPatient: Guilty after eating… I don’t know. Not in the way I think you mean, like because I ate too much or because of my weight or anything. Then I think, “Why did I do that?” But it’s more confusion than guilt, I guess. And on the days I hardly eat, I don’t really feel proud or guilty either. It’s just like… the day disappears and then I realize I didn’t take care of myself very well.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever worry about gaining weight?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t really worry about gaining weight. That’s not what’s in my head when I’m eating or not eating.\n\nIt’s more that my eating has gotten all turned around for months now, and that’s actually part of why I came in. Some days I just sort of pick at toast and coffee and don’t have any real interest in anything else, and then other days I’ll find myself standing in the bakery aisle buying cookies or pastries or whatever sweet thing is there, even though I don’t really enjoy it when I get home. It’s not because I’m scared of gaining weight or trying to control my body. It just feels… off, like I’m not eating in a normal way for me anymore.\n\nDoctor: Do you notice that your eating habits change in response to how you're feeling emotionally?\n\nPatient: Yes, I think so, though it’s not always clear to me in the moment. It’s more like my eating follows whatever state I’m in without me deciding it. When I’m feeling really low or shut down, food just sort of drops away. I’ll have toast and coffee, maybe pick at something, and then suddenly it’s late and I realize I never made a real meal.\n\nAnd then other times, when I feel unsettled or empty or just… I don’t know, not right, I’ll end up in the bakery aisle buying sweets. Cookies, pastries, things like that. It’s almost automatic, like I’m trying to fix something, but then I get home and I don’t even really enjoy them. They just sit there, or I eat some and it doesn’t do much.\n\nThat’s part of why I came in, honestly. It’s been going on for months, this back and forth where some days I barely eat and other days I’m buying sweet things I don’t even want. It doesn’t feel normal for me.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your eating habits are affected by stress?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think stress probably does affect it, though it’s hard for me to separate stress from just feeling low or off, because it all kind of runs together lately.\n\nWhat I notice is that my eating has been really turned around for months now. Some days, if I’m tense or overwhelmed, I don’t really eat properly at all. I’ll just have toast and coffee and sort of pick at it, and then I realize hours later that I never made an actual meal. It doesn’t feel like a choice exactly—it’s more like food just becomes too much effort, or I don’t have any interest in it.\n\nThen other days, especially when I feel unsettled, I’ll end up in the bakery aisle buying sweets—cookies or pastries or things like that. And in the moment it feels like I’m doing something, like maybe it’ll help somehow, but then I get home and I don’t even really enjoy them. Sometimes they just sit there, or I eat a little and it doesn’t feel like anything.\n\nSo yes, I’d say stress is mixed up in it. It’s one of the reasons I came in, because this isn’t how I normally eat, and it’s been going on long enough that I’m worried about it.\n\nDoctor: Are there any areas of your life, such as work, relationships, or daily activities, that you feel are being affected by your current eating habits?\n\nPatient: Yes… it has, more than I wanted to admit at first. I still get through the basic things for my aunt — I make sure she’s taken care of, and I don’t just abandon what has to be done — but it all feels so much harder than it used to.\n\nIt’s like, if I haven’t eaten right or I’ve just had coffee and toast and then nothing, I don’t have the steadiness for the rest of the day. And then on the days where I’ve bought sweets and feel bad or just kind of weighed down by it, I don’t really feel any better either. So things pile up.\n\nI’ve missed church planning calls, which I normally would not do. I’ve put off some fair projects I said I’d help with. And there are things around town or with my aunt where I’ve had to ask neighbors to cover for me, things I used to just handle without making a big deal out of it. That’s embarrassing, honestly, because I’ve always tried to be dependable.\n\nSo yes, I’d say it’s affecting caregiving and just staying involved in town. I’m still doing the basics, but it feels like I’m dragging myself through them, and I’m not keeping up the way I used to.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like eating or buying food is a way for you to cope with difficult emotions?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think it is, at least sometimes. Not in a way that feels very deliberate, though. It’s not like I say to myself, “I’m upset, so I’m going to buy food.” It’s more like I’m already feeling bad or empty or restless, and then I find myself standing in the bakery aisle looking at sweets, and I’ve already put cookies or pastries in the basket before I’ve really thought it through.\n\nThat’s part of why I came in, because my eating has gotten all turned around for months now. Some days I barely want anything and I’m just picking at toast and coffee, and then other days I’m buying sweet things I don’t even really enjoy once I get them home. It feels like I’m trying to do something to make myself feel better, but it doesn’t actually work. It just makes me feel strange about myself afterward, like I don’t understand why I did it.\n\nDoctor: Do you think that when you buy or eat sweets, you're trying to improve your mood?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think that’s probably what’s happening, or at least part of what’s happening. Cookies, pastries, whatever looks like it might be comforting, I guess. But then I get them home and I don’t even really enjoy them. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yes… I think so. I mean, nobody has sat me down exactly and said, “Your eating habits have changed,” but I think people can tell something is off. It’s hard not to notice, I guess, because it’s been going on for months now.

That’s really one of the reasons I came in. My eating feels like it’s gotten completely turned around. Some days I’ll just have coffee and maybe pick at toast, and I’ll sort of tell myself I’ll eat something proper later, but then later doesn’t really happen. And then other days I do the opposite thing, where I end up in the bakery aisle buying sweets — cookies, pastries, things like that — even though when I get them home I don’t really enjoy them the way I thought I would.

So I think people around me probably notice that I’m not eating normally, or that I don’t seem like myself overall. I try not to make a big display of it, but it’s been hard to hide because it’s become such a strange pattern.

And then other days I’ll be standing in the bakery aisle buying sweets, like pastries or cookies, things I don’t even really enjoy once I get them home. It feels strange, like I’m not eating in any normal way anymore.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost any weight over these months?\n\nPatient: I’m not completely sure about the number, honestly. I haven’t been keeping track on a scale like I probably should. It’s more that my eating has been so… uneven. Like I said, some days I’ll hardly eat anything, just toast and coffee, and then I realize the whole day’s gone by like that. And then other days I’m buying sweets in the bakery aisle—cookies, pastries, things like that—even though when I get home I don’t really want them or enjoy them.\n\nSo I don’t know if I’ve lost weight exactly, but my appetite and the way I eat have definitely changed for months now. It doesn’t feel normal for me.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever think about your weight or body shape when you're deciding what or how much to eat?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think it’s really about my weight or how I look. It’s more like… I just don’t feel interested in food some days, or I forget, or nothing sounds worth making. And then other times I’ll buy sweet things almost automatically, but not because I’m trying to change my shape or because I’m worried about calories. Then I think, “Why did I do that?” But it’s more confusion than guilt, I guess. And on the days I hardly eat, I don’t really feel proud or guilty either. It’s just like… the day disappears and then I realize I didn’t take care of myself very well.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever worry about gaining weight?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t really worry about gaining weight. That’s not what’s in my head when I’m eating or not eating.\n\nIt’s more that my eating has gotten all turned around for months now, and that’s actually part of why I came in. Some days I just sort of pick at toast and coffee and don’t have any real interest in anything else, and then other days I’ll find myself standing in the bakery aisle buying cookies or pastries or whatever sweet thing is there, even though I don’t really enjoy it when I get home. It’s not because I’m scared of gaining weight or trying to control my body. It just feels… off, like I’m not eating in a normal way for me anymore.\n\nDoctor: Do you notice that your eating habits change in response to how you're feeling emotionally?\n\nPatient: Yes, I think so, though it’s not always clear to me in the moment. It’s more like my eating follows whatever state I’m in without me deciding it. When I’m feeling really low or shut down, food just sort of drops away. I’ll have toast and coffee, maybe pick at something, and then suddenly it’s late and I realize I never made a real meal.\n\nAnd then other times, when I feel unsettled or empty or just… I don’t know, not right, I’ll end up in the bakery aisle buying sweets. Cookies, pastries, things like that. It’s almost automatic, like I’m trying to fix something, but then I get home and I don’t even really enjoy them. They just sit there, or I eat some and it doesn’t do much.\n\nThat’s part of why I came in, honestly. It’s been going on for months, this back and forth where some days I barely eat and other days I’m buying sweet things I don’t even want. It doesn’t feel normal for me.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your eating habits are affected by stress?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think stress probably does affect it, though it’s hard for me to separate stress from just feeling low or off, because it all kind of runs together lately.\n\nWhat I notice is that my eating has been really turned around for months now. Some days, if I’m tense or overwhelmed, I don’t really eat properly at all. I’ll just have toast and coffee and sort of pick at it, and then I realize hours later that I never made an actual meal. It doesn’t feel like a choice exactly—it’s more like food just becomes too much effort, or I don’t have any interest in it.\n\nThen other days, especially when I feel unsettled, I’ll end up in the bakery aisle buying sweets—cookies or pastries or things like that. And in the moment it feels like I’m doing something, like maybe it’ll help somehow, but then I get home and I don’t even really enjoy them. Sometimes they just sit there, or I eat a little and it doesn’t feel like anything.\n\nSo yes, I’d say stress is mixed up in it. It’s one of the reasons I came in, because this isn’t how I normally eat, and it’s been going on long enough that I’m worried about it.\n\nDoctor: Are there any areas of your life, such as work, relationships, or daily activities, that you feel are being affected by your current eating habits?\n\nPatient: Yes… it has, more than I wanted to admit at first. I still get through the basic things for my aunt — I make sure she’s taken care of, and I don’t just abandon what has to be done — but it all feels so much harder than it used to.\n\nIt’s like, if I haven’t eaten right or I’ve just had coffee and toast and then nothing, I don’t have the steadiness for the rest of the day. And then on the days where I’ve bought sweets and feel bad or just kind of weighed down by it, I don’t really feel any better either. So things pile up.\n\nI’ve missed church planning calls, which I normally would not do. I’ve put off some fair projects I said I’d help with. And there are things around town or with my aunt where I’ve had to ask neighbors to cover for me, things I used to just handle without making a big deal out of it. That’s embarrassing, honestly, because I’ve always tried to be dependable.\n\nSo yes, I’d say it’s affecting caregiving and just staying involved in town. I’m still doing the basics, but it feels like I’m dragging myself through them, and I’m not keeping up the way I used to.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like eating or buying food is a way for you to cope with difficult emotions?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think it is, at least sometimes. Not in a way that feels very deliberate, though. It’s not like I say to myself, “I’m upset, so I’m going to buy food.” It’s more like I’m already feeling bad or empty or restless, and then I find myself standing in the bakery aisle looking at sweets, and I’ve already put cookies or pastries in the basket before I’ve really thought it through.\n\nThat’s part of why I came in, because my eating has gotten all turned around for months now. Some days I barely want anything and I’m just picking at toast and coffee, and then other days I’m buying sweet things I don’t even really enjoy once I get them home. It feels like I’m trying to do something to make myself feel better, but it doesn’t actually work. It just makes me feel strange about myself afterward, like I don’t understand why I did it.\n\nDoctor: Do you think that when you buy or eat sweets, you're trying to improve your mood?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think that’s probably what’s happening, or at least part of what’s happening. Some days I’m barely eating, just sort of picking at toast and coffee and not making a real meal, and then other days I end up standing in the bakery aisle buying sweets. Cookies, pastries, whatever looks like it might be comforting, I guess. But then I get them home and I don’t even really enjoy them. It’s like I thought it would help, but it doesn’t land the way I expected.\n\nSo yes, I think maybe I’m trying to improve my mood, but it doesn’t really work. It just leaves me feeling more confused about why I did it.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you're losing control when you're eating?\n\nPatient: Yes… sometimes it does feel that way, though maybe not like I’m sitting there eating and can’t physically stop. That’s not what’s in my head when I’m eating or not eating.\n\nIt’s more that my eating has gotten all turned around for months now, and that’s actually part of why I came in. Some days I just sort of pick at toast and coffee and don’t have any real interest in anything else, and then other days I’ll find myself standing in the bakery aisle buying cookies or pastries or whatever sweet thing is there, even though I don’t really enjoy it when I get home. It’s not because I’m scared of gaining weight or trying to control my body. It just feels… off, like I’m not eating in a normal way for me anymore.\n\nDoctor: Do you notice that your eating habits change in response to how you're feeling emotionally?\n\nPatient: Yes, I think so, though it’s not always clear to me in the moment. It’s more like my eating follows whatever state I’m in without me deciding it. When I’m feeling really low or shut down, food just sort of drops away. I’ll have toast and coffee, maybe pick at something, and then suddenly it’s late and I realize I never made a real meal.\n\nAnd then other times, when I feel unsettled or empty or just… I don’t know, not right, I’ll end up in the bakery aisle buying sweets. Cookies, pastries, things like that. It’s almost automatic, like I’m trying to fix something, but then I get home and I don’t even really enjoy them. They just sit there, or I eat some and it doesn’t do much.\n\nThat’s part of why I came in, honestly. It’s been going on for months, this back and forth where some days I barely eat and other days I’m buying sweet things I don’t even want. It doesn’t feel normal for me.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your eating habits are affected by stress?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think stress probably does affect it, though it’s hard for me to separate stress from just feeling low or off, because it all kind of runs together lately.\n\nWhat I notice is that my eating has been really turned around for months now. Some days, if I’m tense or overwhelmed, I don’t really eat properly at all. I’ll just have toast and coffee and sort of pick at it, and then I realize hours later that I never made an actual meal. It doesn’t feel like a choice exactly—it’s more like food just becomes too much effort, or I don’t have any interest in it.\n\nThen other days, especially when I feel unsettled, I’ll end up in the bakery aisle buying sweets—cookies or pastries or things like that. And in the moment it feels like I’m doing something, like maybe it’ll help somehow, but then I get home and I don’t even really enjoy them. Sometimes they just sit there, or I eat a little and it doesn’t feel like anything.\n\nSo yes, I’d say stress is mixed up in it. It’s one of the reasons I came in, because this isn’t how I normally eat, and it’s been going on long enough that I’m worried about it.\n\nDoctor: Are there any areas of your life, such as work, relationships, or daily activities, that you feel are being affected by your current eating habits?\n\nPatient: Yes… it has, more than I wanted to admit at first. I still get through the basic things for my aunt — I make sure she’s taken care of, and I don’t just abandon what has to be done — but it all feels so much harder than it used to.\n\nIt’s like, if I haven’t eaten right or I’ve just had coffee and toast and then nothing, I don’t have the steadiness for the rest of the day. And then on the days where I’ve bought sweets and feel bad or just kind of weighed down by it, I don’t really feel any better either. So things pile up.\n\nI’ve missed church planning calls, which I normally would not do. I’ve put off some fair projects I said I’d help with. And there are things around town or with my aunt where I’ve had to ask neighbors to cover for me, things I used to just handle without making a big deal out of it. That’s embarrassing, honestly, because I’ve always tried to be dependable.\n\nSo yes, I’d say it’s affecting caregiving and just staying involved in town. I’m still doing the basics, but it feels like I’m dragging myself through them, and I’m not keeping up the way I used to.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like eating or buying food is a way for you to cope with difficult emotions?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think it is, at least sometimes. Not in a way that feels very deliberate, though. It’s not like I say to myself, “I’m upset, so I’m going to buy food.” It’s more like I’m already feeling bad or empty or restless, and then I find myself standing in the bakery aisle looking at sweets, and I’ve already put cookies or pastries in the basket before I’ve really thought it through.\n\nThat’s part of why I came in, because my eating has gotten all turned around for months now. Some days I barely want anything and I’m just picking at toast and coffee, and then other days I’m buying sweet things I don’t even really enjoy once I get them home. It feels like I’m trying to do something to make myself feel better, but it doesn’t actually work. It just makes me feel strange about myself afterward, like I don’t understand why I did it.\n\nDoctor: Do you think that when you buy or eat sweets, you're trying to improve your mood?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think that’s probably what’s happening, or at least part of what’s happening. It doesn’t feel very clear in the moment, like I’m not standing there thinking, “This will fix my mood.” But I do think I’m trying to feel something different, maybe just for a minute.\n\nThat’s really why I came in, because my eating has gotten so turned around for months now. Some days I’m barely eating, just sort of picking at toast and coffee and not making a real meal, and then other days I end up standing in the bakery aisle buying sweets. Cookies, pastries, whatever looks like it might be comforting, I guess. But then I get them home and I don’t even really enjoy them. It’s like I thought it would help, but it doesn’t land the way I expected.\n\nSo yes, I think maybe I’m trying to improve my mood, but it doesn’t really work. It just leaves me feeling more confused about why I did it.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you're losing control when you're eating?\n\nPatient: Yes… sometimes it does feel that way, though maybe not like I’m sitting there eating and can’t physically stop. It’s more the whole thing leading up to it feels out of my hands.\n\nI mean, that’s part of why I came in. My eating has just gotten so turned around these past months. Some days I’ll hardly eat anything proper — just coffee and a piece of toast, maybe picking at something later but not making a real meal. And then another day I’ll find myself in the bakery aisle, buying sweets like cookies or pastries, and it feels like I’ve already done it before I’ve really decided to.\n\nThen when I get home, I don’t even enjoy them much. I’ll look at what I bought and think, “Why did I do that?” So yes, in that sense, it feels like I’m not quite in control of it. Not every single time, but enough that it bothers me.\n\nDoctor: Do you notice your eating habits changing when you're feeling sad or anxious?\n\nPatient: Yes, I do think it changes with how I’m feeling. It’s not always neat enough that I can say, “I felt sad at noon, then I ate this at one,” but when I’m low or wound up inside, my eating gets… strange. It’s like I lose the normal rhythm of it.\n\nThat’s actually a big part of why I came in, because for months now it’s been all turned around. Some days I don’t want much of anything, and I’ll just pick at toast and drink coffee, and then I realize the whole day has gone by and I haven’t really eaten a proper meal. And other days, especially when I feel empty or unsettled, I’ll end up in the bakery aisle buying sweets — cookies or pastries — like they’re going to help somehow.\n\nBut then I get them home and I don’t even really enjoy them. Sometimes I just look at them and feel worse, like I don’t understand what I was trying to do. So yes… when I’m sad or anxious, my eating definitely seems to shift, but not in a way that feels healthy or satisfying.\n\nDoctor: Are there people in your life who have noticed a change in your eating habits or overall well-being?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think so. I mean, nobody has sat me down exactly and said, “Your eating habits have changed,” but I think people can tell something is off. It’s hard not to notice, I guess, because it’s been going on for months now.\n\nThat’s really one of the reasons I came in. My eating feels like it’s gotten completely turned around. Some days I’ll just have coffee and maybe pick at toast, and I’ll sort of tell myself I’ll eat something proper later, but then later doesn’t really happen. And then other days I do the opposite thing, where I end up in the bakery aisle buying sweets — cookies, pastries, things like that — even though when I get them home I don’t really enjoy them the way I thought I would.\n\nSo I think people around me probably notice that I’m not eating normally, or that I don’t seem like myself overall. Yes… I think that’s close to it. I don’t know if I’d say I’m thinking, “I’m going to lose control of my emotions, so I should eat,” not that clearly. It’s more muddled than that. But when I feel like I’m getting overwhelmed inside, my eating is one of the first things that seems to go off track.

That’s part of why I came in, really. For months now it’s been all turned around. Some days I barely eat anything that feels like a real meal — just coffee, maybe toast, and then I keep putting off eating properly. And then other days I’ll be standing in the bakery aisle buying cookies or pastries, like some part of me thinks that will settle something down.

But then I get home and I don’t even really enjoy them. I’ll eat some or just look at them and feel foolish, like, why did I do that? So yes, I think it does connect to feeling emotionally out of control, but not in a planned way. It feels more like my eating gets pulled along by whatever is happening inside me.
